46	1. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**
47	   `0.47.0` introduced the convention (single-field leaf names
48	   its field `value`, declares the flag via `#[arg(long = "…")]`,
49	   so consumers read `args.<leaf>.value` not `args.<leaf>.<leaf>`)
50	   on the new `squash` leaf. Sweep the pre-existing single-field
51	   leaves to match: `scope`, `repo`, `dry_run`, `private`,
52	   `account`, `config`, `use_template` + their consumers
53	   (`init.rs`, tests).
54	1. **Drop `-R`/`--repo` from `CommonArgs` once `-s`/`--scope` is
55	   established.**
56	   - `0.49.0-2.2` kept `-R` (single path) alongside the new `-s`
57	     (roles, composes with `-R` as workspace root) as a
58	     backwards-compat alias for the migration period.
59	   - Once users / scripts have moved, drop `-R` — `--scope` then
60	     covers both path and role forms (`parse_scope` already
61	     handles both; only `parse_scope_roles` would go away).
62	1. **`-s <path>` and `-s <path>,roles` workspace-root override.**
63	   - Today `-s` is keyword-only (`parse_scope_roles` rejects
64	     paths, pointing users at `-R`).
65	   - Future: have `parse_scope_roles` accept a comma list mixing
66	     one path + role keywords, where the path is the workspace
67	     root.
68	   - `vc-x1 chid -s ../foo,bot,code` would resolve to `[../foo,
69	     ../foo/.claude]` (today `-R ../foo -s code,bot`).
70	   - Decisions to make at design time: comma-list syntax, error
71	     cases (multiple paths, path without roles vs
72	     path-as-`Scope::Single`), and whether to also accept bare
73	     `-s <path>` as a synonym for `-R <path>`.
74	1. **por/dual parity + bidirectional conversion.** Make
75	   `por` and `dual` first-class equals (dual is primary
76	   today, por bolted on); add `por → dual` / `dual → por`
77	   conversion. Builds on the `--scope` rollout below.

120	Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-n`/`--commits`, and `-R`/`--repo`
121	take precedence over positional arguments.
122	
123	### Multi-repo queries
124	
125	The `-R`/`--repo` flag can be repeated or comma-separated to query
126	multiple repos at once. When multiple repos are given, output is
127	labeled with bold `=== path ===` headers by default:
128	
129	```
130	vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude      # repeated flag
131	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude         # comma-separated
132	vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude   # works with all read-only subcommands
133	```
134	
135	Control the label between repos with `-l`/`--label` and `-L`/`--no-label`:
136	
137	```
138	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default label: === path ===
139	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l "---"   # custom label:  --- path ---
140	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L         # no label (raw output)
141	```
142	
143	Examples:
144	
145	```
146	$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude
147	=== . ===
148	kwoyvposvsmv
149	
150	=== .claude ===
151	zzpksklxyrnw
152	
153	$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude -L
154	kwoyvposvsmv
155	zzpksklxyrnw
156	
157	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude
158	=== . ===
159	kwoyvposvsmv
160	
161	=== .claude ===
162	zzpksklxyrnw
163	
164	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L
165	kwoyvposvsmv
166	zzpksklxyrnw
167	
168	$ vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude
169	=== . ===
170	kwoyvposvsmv eae175c3c1b5 (no description set)
171	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
172	ssokyszzwsxw eeb15bc6d839 Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
173	
174	=== .claude ===
175	zzpksklxyrnw 2388185e42ee (no description set)
176	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
177	slmkmroqtqtp 1c33ccaa567d Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
178	
179	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L
180	kwoyvposvsmv
181	zzpksklxyrnw
182	
183	$ vc-x1 desc -r @- -R .,.claude
184	=== . ===
185	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
186	
187	    Move completed items [1]-[13] and their references from todo.md to done.md.
188	    Replace completed jj-organization todo with new items.
189	
190	    ochid: tzupykyyvnrp
191	
192	=== .claude ===
193	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
194	
195	    Session: reviewed and committed notes reorganization for 0.19.1.
196	
197	    ochid: pqxtxxpnsmot
198	
199	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l "---"
200	--- . ---
201	kwoyvposvsmv
202	
203	--- .claude ---
204	zzpksklxyrnw
205	```
206	
207	Most decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,
208	`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters
209	like `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes
210	won't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).
211	
212	With a single repo (or no `-R`), no label is printed — backward
213	compatible with previous behavior. Multi-repo is supported for
214	`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo.
215	
216	### validate-desc
217	
218	Read-only scan of commit descriptions against the other repo. The other
219	repo is read from `.vc-config.toml` (`workspace.other-repo`) by default,
```

```
1	//! `CommonArgs` — the shared arg set for the read-only commit-query
2	//! subcommands (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`): a `REVISION` /
3	//! `COMMITS` positional pair, `-r`/`--revision`, `-R`/`--repo`,
4	//! `-s`/`--scope`, `-n`/`--commits`, and the `-l`/`-L` inter-repo
5	//! label pair.
6	//!
7	//! A "bundle" in the inline-fields sense (cf. `provision_bundle`'s
8	//! flatten-of-leaves form): the fields aren't extracted into per-flag
9	//! leaves because none of them is reused outside these four — if one
10	//! ever is (e.g. a future command wants just `--revision`), extract
11	//! that field into a leaf and `#[command(flatten)]` it here. Consumers
12	//! flatten this and add their own extra `#[arg]` fields (`list`'s
13	//! `-w`/`--width`, `show`'s `-f`/`--files`). See
14	//! [options_flags](README.md) for shared architecture.
15	
16	use std::path::PathBuf;
17	
18	use clap::Args;
19	
20	use crate::scope::{Scope, parse_scope_roles};
21	
22	/// Shared CLI args for the read-only commit-query subcommands —
23	/// see [Bundle](README.md#architecture).
24	///
25	/// - `pos_rev` / `pos_count` — the `REVISION` / `COMMITS` positionals
26	///   (`common::resolve_spec` reconciles them with `-r` / `-n`).
27	/// - `revision` — `-r` / `--revision` (default `@`); `..` notation is
28	///   parsed downstream by `common::parse_dot_rev`.
29	/// - `repo` / `scope` — `-R PATH` overrides the workspace root, `-s
30	///   code|bot|code,bot` selects sides; they compose
31	///   (`common::resolve_repos`). Defaults preserve today's behavior:
32	///   no flag → `[.]`, `-R foo` alone → `[foo]`. `-s` alone resolves
33	///   against `find_workspace_root()`; `-R + -s` resolves against the
34	///   `-R` path. `-s` is keyword-only today (path-via-`-s` and the
35	///   `-s <path>,roles` workspace-root override are planned — see
36	///   `notes/todo.md`).
37	/// - `limit` — `-n` / `--commits`, caps the output.
38	/// - `label` / `no_label` — `-l` / `--label` (default `===`) and
39	///   `-L` / `--no-label`; `common::resolve_header` combines them.
40	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
41	pub struct CommonArgs {
42	    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
43	    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
44	    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
45	
46	    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)
47	    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
48	    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
49	
50	    /// Revision to query
51	    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
52	    pub revision: String,
53	
54	    /// Workspace root or single jj repo path [default: .]
55	    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
56	    pub repo: Option<PathBuf>,
57	
58	    /// Side(s) to query; composes with --repo as workspace root
59	    #[arg(
60	        short = 's',
61	        long = "scope",
62	        value_name = "code|bot|code,bot",
63	        value_parser = parse_scope_roles
64	    )]
65	    pub scope: Option<Scope>,
66	
67	    /// Number of commits to show
68	    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "commits", value_name = "COMMITS")]
69	    pub limit: Option<usize>,
70	
71	    /// Custom label decoration between repos
72	    #[arg(
73	        short = 'l',
74	        long = "label",
75	        value_name = "TEXT",
76	        allow_hyphen_values = true,
77	        default_value = "==="
78	    )]
79	    pub label: String,
80	
81	    /// Suppress label between repos
82	    #[arg(short = 'L', long = "no-label")]
83	    pub no_label: bool,
84	}
85	
86	impl CommonArgs {
87	    /// Resolve `-R` + `-s` into the concrete repo paths to iterate
88	    /// (delegates to [`crate::common::resolve_repos`]).
89	    ///
90	    /// Bundles the `.as_deref()` / `.as_ref()` conversion ceremony
91	    /// (`Option<PathBuf>` → `Option<&Path>`,
92	    /// `Option<Scope>` → `Option<&Scope>`) into one well-named place
93	    /// so the four subcommand bodies stay clean. See
94	    /// [`../../notes/rust-idioms.md`](../../notes/rust-idioms.md)
95	    /// for why the two fields need different conversion methods
96	    /// (`PathBuf: Deref<Target = Path>`; `Scope` is a plain enum).
97	    pub fn resolve_repos(&self) -> Result<Vec<std::path::PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
98	        crate::common::resolve_repos(self.repo.as_deref(), self.scope.as_ref())
99	    }
100	}
101	
102	impl super::OptionFlagBundle for CommonArgs {}
103	
```

1	# vc-x1
2	
3	- [Overview](#vc-x1)
4	- [Architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md)
5	- [Usage](#usage)
6	  - [Revision shortcuts](#revision-shortcuts)
7	  - [Shell completion](#shell-completion)
8	  - [validate-desc](#validate-desc)
9	  - [fix-desc](#fix-desc)
10	  - [clone](#clone)
11	  - [init](#init)
12	  - [symlink](#symlink)
13	  - [finalize](#finalize)
14	  - [Testing push + finalize](#testing-push--finalize)
15	- [Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers](#cross-repo-linking-with-git-trailers)
16	- [jj Tips for Git Users](#jj-tips-for-git-users)
17	- [Thoughts for the future](#thoughts-for-the-future)
18	- [Contributing](#contributing)
19	- [License](#license)
20	
21	This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
22	We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
23	in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
24	initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
25	made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
26	and one with the conversation with the bot.
27	
28	I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
29	each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
30	of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
31	changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
32	between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
33	evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
34	most importantly **why** the code evolved.
35	
36	The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
37	(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
38	existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
39	own IDE for vc.
40	
41	See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
42	for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
43	doing so and I then created this README.md file.
44	
45	## Usage
46	
47	```
48	vc-x1 list [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # List commits in a jj repo
49	vc-x1 desc [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show full description of a commit
50	vc-x1 chid [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Print changeID(s) for a revision
51	vc-x1 show [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show commit details and diff summary
52	vc-x1 validate-desc [OPTS]                 # Validate commit descriptions
53	vc-x1 fix-desc [OPTS]                     # Fix commit descriptions (dry-run default)
54	vc-x1 clone <REPO> [NAME] [OPTS]          # Clone a dual-repo project
55	vc-x1 init <NAME> [OPTS]                  # Create a new dual-repo project
56	vc-x1 symlink [TARGET] [OPTS]             # Create Claude Code project symlink
57	vc-x1 sync [OPTS]                          # Fetch + sync both repos to their remotes
58	vc-x1 finalize --bookmark <B> [OPTS]       # Squash working copy into target
59	vc-x1 --version                            # Print version
60	vc-x1 --help                           # Print help
```

```
60	vc-x1 --help                           # Print help
61	```
62	
63	**The `..` notation:** dots on the revision show which direction to
64	list commits:
65	
66	- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
67	- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
68	- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
69	
70	COMMITS is the total number of commits to show (x is always included).
71	Without dots, `x` with a count defaults to `x..` (ancestors).
72	For `..x..`, the budget is split: ancestors get the extra on odd counts.
73	
74	```
75	vc-x1 list -r @              # just @ (1 commit)
76	vc-x1 list -r @.. -n 5       # @ + 4 ancestors (5 commits, @ at top)
77	vc-x1 list -r ..@ -n 3       # 2 descendants + @ (3 commits, @ at bottom)
78	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 3     # 1 descendant, @, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
79	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 4     # 1 descendant, @, 2 ancestors (4 commits)
80	```
81	
82	### Shell completion
83	
84	vc-x1 provides tab completion for all subcommands and flags using claps
85	[unstable-dynamic](https://docs.rs/clap_complete/latest/clap_complete/env/struct.CompleteEnv.html)
86	only feature. It is a "simple" implementation and does not handle completing
87	revisions but still useful. To enable it, add one of the following to your shell's startup file:
88	
89	```bash
90	# bash (~/.bashrc)
91	source <(COMPLETE=bash vc-x1)
92	
93	# zsh (~/.zshrc)
94	source <(COMPLETE=zsh vc-x1)
95	
96	# fish (~/.config/fish/config.fish)
97	source (COMPLETE=fish vc-x1 | psub)
98	```
99	
100	Completions are generated dynamically by the binary, so they stay in
101	sync with the installed version automatically.
102	
103	### Positional shorthand
104	
105	REVISION and COMMITS can be given as positional arguments, omitting
106	`-r` and `-n`:
107	
108	```
109	vc-x1 list x                # just x (1 commit)
110	vc-x1 list x 5              # x.. 5 → x + 4 ancestors (5 commits)
111	vc-x1 list x.. 5            # same as above
112	vc-x1 list ..x 3            # 2 descendants + x (3 commits)
113	vc-x1 list ..x.. 3          # 1 descendant, x, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
114	vc-x1 list ..x.. 1          # just x (1 commit)
115	```
116	
117	**Defaults:** with no arguments, REVISION is `@` and COMMITS is 1
118	(just the working copy).
119	
120	Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-n`/`--commits`, and `-R`/`--repo`
121	take precedence over positional arguments.
122	
123	### Multi-repo queries
124	
125	The `-R`/`--repo` flag can be repeated or comma-separated to query
126	multiple repos at once. When multiple repos are given, output is
127	labeled with bold `=== path ===` headers by default:
128	
129	```
130	vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude      # repeated flag
131	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude         # comma-separated
132	vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude   # works with all read-only subcommands
133	```
134	
135	Control the label between repos with `-l`/`--label` and `-L`/`--no-label`:
136	
137	```
138	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default label: === path ===
139	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l "---"   # custom label:  --- path ---
140	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L         # no label (raw output)
141	```
142	
143	Examples:
144	
145	```
146	$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude
147	=== . ===
148	kwoyvposvsmv
149	
150	=== .claude ===
151	zzpksklxyrnw
152	
153	$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude -L
154	kwoyvposvsmv
155	zzpksklxyrnw
156	
157	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude
158	=== . ===
159	kwoyvposvsmv
160	
161	=== .claude ===
162	zzpksklxyrnw
163	
164	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L
165	kwoyvposvsmv
166	zzpksklxyrnw
167	
168	$ vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude
169	=== . ===
170	kwoyvposvsmv eae175c3c1b5 (no description set)
171	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
172	ssokyszzwsxw eeb15bc6d839 Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
173	
174	=== .claude ===
175	zzpksklxyrnw 2388185e42ee (no description set)
176	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)
177	slmkmroqtqtp 1c33ccaa567d Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)
178	
179	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L
180	kwoyvposvsmv
181	zzpksklxyrnw
182	
183	$ vc-x1 desc -r @- -R .,.claude
184	=== . ===
185	pqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
186	
187	    Move completed items [1]-[13] and their references from todo.md to done.md.
188	    Replace completed jj-organization todo with new items.
189	
190	    ochid: tzupykyyvnrp
191	
192	=== .claude ===
193	tzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)
194	
195	    Session: reviewed and committed notes reorganization for 0.19.1.
196	
197	    ochid: pqxtxxpnsmot
198	
199	$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l "---"
200	--- . ---
201	kwoyvposvsmv
202	
203	--- .claude ---
204	zzpksklxyrnw
205	```
206	
207	Most decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,
208	`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters
209	like `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes
210	won't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).
211	
212	With a single repo (or no `-R`), no label is printed — backward
213	compatible with previous behavior. Multi-repo is supported for
214	`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo.
215	
216	### validate-desc
217	
218	Read-only scan of commit descriptions against the other repo. The other
219	repo is read from `.vc-config.toml` (`workspace.other-repo`) by default,
```

works with all read-only subcommands\n```\n\nControl the label between repos with `-l`/`--label` and `-L`/`--no-label`:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default label: === path ===\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l \"---\"   # custom label:  --- path ---\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L         # no label (raw output)\n```\n\nExamples:\n\n```\n$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude\n=== . ===\nkwoyvposvsmv\n\n=== .claude ===\nzzpksklxyrnw\n\n$ vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude -L\nkwoyvposvsmv\nzzpksklxyrnw\n\n$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude\n=== . ===\nkwoyvposvsmv\n\n=== .claude ===\nzzpksklxyrnw\n\n$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L\nkwoyvposvsmv\nzzpksklxyrnw\n\n$ vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude\n=== . ===\nkwoyvposvsmv eae175c3c1b5 (no description set)\npqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)\nssokyszzwsxw eeb15bc6d839 Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)\n\n=== .claude ===\nzzpksklxyrnw 2388185e42ee (no description set)\ntzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)\nslmkmroqtqtp 1c33ccaa567d Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)\n\n$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L\nkwoyvposvsmv\nzzpksklxyrnw\n\n$ vc-x1 desc -r @- -R .,.claude\n=== . ===\npqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)\n\n    Move completed items [1]-[13] and their references from todo.md to done.md.\n    Replace completed jj-organization todo with new items.\n\n    ochid: tzupykyyvnrp\n\n=== .claude ===\ntzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)\n\n    Session: reviewed and committed notes reorganization for 0.19.1.\n\n    ochid: pqxtxxpnsmot\n\n$ vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l \"---\"\n--- . ---\nkwoyvposvsmv\n\n--- .claude ---\nzzpksklxyrnw\n```\n\nMost decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,\n`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters\nlike `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes\nwon't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).\n\nWith a single repo (or no `-R`), no label is printed — backward\ncompatible with previous behavior. Multi-repo is supported for\n`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo.",
  "new_string": "Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-n`/`--commits`, `-R`/`--repo`, and\n`-s`/`--scope` take precedence over positional arguments.\n\n### Multi-repo queries\n\n`-s`/`--scope` selects workspace sides by role keyword; `code` is the\napp repo, `bot` is the `.claude` repo, `code,bot` is both. The\nworkspace root is found by walking up from cwd (the existing\n`find_workspace_root` rule).\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -s code,bot          # both sides of the workspace\nvc-x1 chid -s code              # just the app repo\nvc-x1 chid -s bot               # just the bot repo\nvc-x1 list @.. 3 -s code,bot    # works with chid / desc / list / show\n```\n\n`-R`/`--repo` takes a single path. Used alone it points at one jj\nrepo (workspace lookup is skipped); combined with `-s` it overrides\nthe workspace root for the role lookup:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -R .                  # single repo at .\nvc-x1 chid -R .claude            # single repo at .claude\nvc-x1 chid -R ../other -s code,bot   # both sides of ../other workspace\n```\n\nDefaults preserve prior behavior — no flag → `[.]`, `-R foo` alone\n→ `[foo]`. When multiple repos are resolved (any `-s` that names\nmore than one role), output is labeled with bold `=== path ===`\nheaders by default. Control the label with `-l`/`--label` and\n`-L`/`--no-label`:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -s code,bot            # default label: === path ===\nvc-x1 chid -s code,bot -l \"---\"   # custom label:  --- path ---\nvc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L         # no label (raw output)\n```\n\nExamples:\n\n```\n$ vc-x1 chid -s code,bot\n=== . ===\nkwoyvposvsmv\n\n=== .claude ===\nzzpksklxyrnw\n\n$ vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L\nkwoyvposvsmv\nzzpksklxyrnw\n\n$ vc-x1 list @.. 3 -s code,bot\n=== . ===\nkwoyvposvsmv eae175c3c1b5 (no description set)\npqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)\nssokyszzwsxw eeb15bc6d839 Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)\n\n=== .claude ===\nzzpksklxyrnw 2388185e42ee (no description set)\ntzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes (0.19.1)\nslmkmroqtqtp 1c33ccaa567d Unify .. notation and CLI (0.19.0)\n\n$ vc-x1 desc -r @- -s code,bot\n=== . ===\npqxtxxpnsmot b0c46930a640 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)\n\n    Move completed items [1]-[13] and their references from todo.md to done.md.\n    Replace completed jj-organization todo with new items.\n\n    ochid: tzupykyyvnrp\n\n=== .claude ===\ntzupykyyvnrp 81ce22e34d41 Reorganize notes: move older done items to done.md, update todos (0.19.1)\n\n    Session: reviewed and committed notes reorganization for 0.19.1.\n\n    ochid: pqxtxxpnsmot\n\n$ vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -l \"---\"\n--- . ---\nkwoyvposvsmv\n\n--- .claude ---\nzzpksklxyrnw\n```\n\nMost decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,\n`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters\nlike `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes\nwon't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).\n\nWith a single repo resolved, no label is printed — backward\ncompatible with the no-flag default. Multi-repo (`-s code,bot`) is\nsupported for `chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains\nsingle-repo.\n\n`-s` is keyword-only today. Path-via-`-s` and a comma-list form\nmixing one path with role keywords (e.g. `-s ../foo,code,bot` as a\nworkspace-root-plus-roles shorthand) are planned — see\n[`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md)."
}
```

```
300	items being removed — so `## Todo` carried zombie entries
301	describing work already underway. The fix is a rule, not a
302	one-off cleanup: when a `## Todo` item is picked up — its `##
303	In Progress` ladder created, or an existing ladder *re-scoped*
304	to absorb it — the entry is deleted in the same commit. `## In
305	Progress` is the sole record until close-out, then it moves to
306	`## Done`. A `## Todo` entry that duplicates current In-Progress
307	work is a process bug; the pre-commit checklist gets a
308	catch-line for it.
309	
310	## feat: chid+co -s/--scope flag (0.49.0-2.2)
311	
312	The code half of the `0.49.0-2` rollout (`0.49.0-2.3` will sweep
313	the docs). Replaces today's `-R .,.claude` / `-R . -R .claude`
314	multi-repo forms with a roles flag (`-s code | bot | code,bot`),
315	keeps `-R` for single-path operation, and lets the two compose
316	as a workspace-root override. See `### Cycle re-scope`'s
317	`--scope` bullet for the wider plan; the two `###` subsections
318	below capture the design pivots that landed in this commit.
319	
320	### Design: `-R` and `-s` compose rather than conflict
321	
322	The chores plan had `-R` dropped, with `--scope=<path>` covering
323	single-repo via `Scope::Single`. On review the design walked
324	through three iterations:
325	
326	- **Drop `-R`.** `--scope=./foo` covers single-path via
327	  `Scope::Single`; one flag, the `./` prefix disambiguates from
328	  the role keywords.
329	- **Mutually-exclusive `-R` + `-s`.** Keep `-R` for paths, add
330	  `-s` for roles, `conflicts_with` between them. Familiar `-R`
331	  preserved; redundant on the path side.
332	- **Composing `-R` + `-s`** (chosen). `-R` overrides the
333	  workspace root (replaces `find_workspace_root()`), `-s`
334	  selects sides (replaces `default_scope(...)`). Together:
335	  `-R <ws> -s <roles>` resolves the roles within `<ws>`. Each
336	  flag alone reads as today's behavior: `-R foo` → `[foo]`, no
337	  flag → `[.]`. The net-new expressivity is the combined form
338	  (e.g. `vc-x1 chid -R ../foo -s bot` → `[../foo/.claude]`).
339	  `-s` is keyword-only today (`parse_scope_roles` rejects paths
340	  with a hint at `-R`); `-s <path>` and the `-s <path>,roles`
341	  workspace-root override are queued as `## Todo` (a future
342	  `Scope::RolesAt { root, sides }` variant, probably). "Drop
343	  `-R` once `-s` is established" is a separate `## Todo` for
344	  after the migration period — kept for backwards-compat now.
345	
346	### Design: `CommonArgs::resolve_repos(&self)` helper
347	
348	`common::resolve_repos(repo, scope)` takes `Option<&Path>` +
349	`Option<&Scope>` — the standard "borrowed unsized" convention
350	(`&Path`, not `&PathBuf`). At each call site that produces an
351	asymmetric `c.repo.as_deref()` / `c.scope.as_ref()` pair — four
352	times across the subcommand bodies, with another four to come in
353	`0.49.0-3..-6`'s `TryFrom<&XxxArgs>` impls. A method on
354	`CommonArgs` localizes the conversion ceremony to one place so
355	callers read `c.resolve_repos()?`, and a new
356	`notes/rust-idioms.md` carries the `as_deref` vs `as_ref`
357	explainer the doc-comment links to. The free function stays as
358	the reusable primitive (a future `finalize --scope` /
359	`push --scope` calls it directly, not through `CommonArgs`).
```

108	## Commit Message Style
109	
110	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
111	a version suffix:
112	
113	```
114	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
115	```
116	
117	- **Title**: ≤50 chars (the "50" of the git 50/72 rule), a short
118	  summary of *what* changed. The version suffix counts toward the
119	  50 — the descriptive part gets whatever's left after `<type>: `
120	  and ` (<version>)`, so favor terse phrasings (`port X to Context`
121	  over `X → Context + XParams`) when the names run long. Common
122	  types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
123	- **Body line width**: wrap every body line at ≤72 cols (the "72"
124	  of the 50/72 rule); bullet continuations indent two spaces.
125	- **App-repo body**: short intro paragraph (1–3 sentences), then a
126	  terse bullet list — one bullet per file changed, the file plus a
127	  one-line gist (e.g. `README.md: new Overview intro`). This list
128	  **is the source of truth** for the cycle's mechanical change
129	  record; the chores section carries the narrative + design, not a
130	  copy of it. Keep bullets terse — a scannable index, not prose;
131	  promote any "why" to a chores `###` subsection.
132	- **Session-repo body**: terse intro + a few session-activity
133	  bullets. Doesn't need to mirror chores since it describes
134	  in-session work, not code changes.
135	- Examples:
136	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
137	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
138	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
139	
140	## Pre-commit Requirements
141	
142	### User approval
143	
144	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
145	user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
146	them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
147	pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
```

329	### Chores section content — no edit list; git is the record
330	
331	A chores section is: a `Commits:` line (first line under the
332	header — see below), a short intro paragraph (what changed and
333	*why*, a notch above file-list granularity), and any `###`
334	design subsections. It does **not** carry a per-file edit list —
335	that lives in the commit message body, which is the source of
336	truth for "what changed mechanically" (immutable, `git
337	show`-able, naturally scoped to the commit). The chores section
338	is the source of truth for the design thinking; the two
339	cross-link, neither restates the other.
340	
341	When the intro starts wanting to explain a mechanism,
342	hypothesis, or wrinkle, don't inflate it — promote that to its
343	own `###` subsection inside the same `chores-NN.md`. If the
344	wrinkle is a live design concern (something that *should*
345	change, not just be recorded), also add a `notes/todo.md` item
346	with a `[N]` ref pointing at that subsection (todo→chores is the
347	normal ref direction).
348	
349	**Why:** a chores edit list and the commit body were specified
350	to be the same content in two places — and detail written twice
351	drifts. Git owns the mechanical record; chores owns the
352	narrative; `Commits:` links them.
353	
354	### Chores commit references
355	
356	The first line under a chores section header is a `Commits:`
357	line citing the git commit(s) that section records:
358	
359	```
360	## refactor: port push to Context (0.48.0-6)
361	
362	Commits: [[3]]
363	
```

624	### Doc comments on every file, function, and method
625	
626	Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
627	function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
628	one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
629	comment exists, not that it be long.
630	
631	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
632	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
633	module / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments
634	inside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a
635	non-obvious WHY.
636	
637	**Shape:** short intro (1–3 sentences, shorter is better), then
638	a `-` bullet list for any structured details. Avoid long prose
639	paragraphs — they read as a wall of text and hide the structure
640	that bullets make scannable. Same shape applies to:
641	
642	- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.
643	- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.
644	- Todo entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an item needs more than
645	  one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).
646	
647	**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive
648	`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets
649	into running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on
650	any field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on
651	its own line in the rendered help.
652	
653	### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls (Rust)
654	
655	Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`,
656	`.unwrap_or_default()`, or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing
657	`// OK: …` comment that justifies why the call is acceptable.
658	
```

> AGENT

Chores-10 paragraph updated.

Now CLAUDE.md suggestions. The Shape rule (line 637–645) is already in place but lives under `### Doc comments on every file, function, and method`, which buries it. Three places in CLAUDE.md describe what amounts to the same intro+bullets shape:

| Location | What it covers |
|---|---|
| **L637 Shape rule** | Doc comments + chores + todo |
| **L117–134 Commit Message Style bullets** | Commit body (intro + file-by-file bullets) |
| **L329–339 Chores section content** | Chores intro + design `###`s; "no per-file edit list" |

Concrete proposals:

### 1. Expand the Shape rule to cover commits + Done (smallest fix)

**L637–645 change** — replace:

```
**Shape:** short intro (1–3 sentences, shorter is better), then
a `-` bullet list for any structured details. Avoid long prose
paragraphs — they read as a wall of text and hide the structure
that bullets make scannable. Same shape applies to:

- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.
- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.
- Todo entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an item needs more than
  one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).
```

with:

```
**Shape:** short intro (1–3 sentences) that explains the *why*
or the high-level *what*, then a `-` bullet list for the
details. Wrap body lines at ≤72 cols (bullet continuations
indent two spaces). Avoid long prose paragraphs — they read as
a wall of text and hide the structure that bullets make
scannable. Same shape applies to:

- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.
- Commit message bodies (both app-repo and session-repo). The
  50-col title rule is the commit-specific add-on; see
  [Commit Message Style](#commit-message-style).
- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.
- Todo and Done entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an entry needs
  more than one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).

Bullet *content* differs by site: commit bodies use **file-by-file**
bullets (the mechanical edit list — source of truth for what
changed); chores / todo / done use **conceptual** bullets (design
points, structural details — not a copy of the commit's edit list).
```

### 2. Slim the Commit Message Style bullets to defer to Shape

**L117–134 change** — replace the four bullets (`Title`, `Body line width`, `App-repo body`, `Session-repo body`) with three bullets:

```
- **Title**: ≤50 chars (the "50" of the git 50/72 rule), a short
  summary of *what* changed. The version suffix counts toward the
  50 — the descriptive part gets whatever's left after `<type>: `
  and ` (<version>)`, so favor terse phrasings (`port X to Context`
  over `X → Context + XParams`) when the names run long. Common
  types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
- **Body**: intro + bullets shape (the "72" of the 50/72 rule
  applies; see [the Shape rule](#doc-comments-on-every-file-function-and-method)).
  App-repo bullets are **file-by-file** — one bullet per file
  changed, the file plus a one-line gist (e.g.
  `README.md: new Overview intro`). This list **is the source of
  truth** for the cycle's mechanical change record; chores
  carries the narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
- **Session-repo body**: same shape; bullets describe in-session
  activity rather than code changes.
```

### 3. Cross-link from the Chores section content rule

**L331–333 tweak** — change the phrasing so it points at the Shape rule instead of re-defining:

Current:
```
A chores section is: a `Commits:` line (first line under the
header — see below), a short intro paragraph (what changed and
*why*, a notch above file-list granularity), and any `###`
design subsections.
```

Proposed:
```
A chores section is: a `Commits:` line (first line under the
header — see below), then the intro + bullets
[Shape](#doc-comments-on-every-file-function-and-method)
(intro explains the *why* at a notch above file-list
granularity; bullets are **conceptual** design / structural
points, never a per-file edit list), and any `###` design
subsections.
```

95	## jj Basics
96	
97	- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
98	- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
99	- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
100	- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
101	- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
102	  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
103	- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
104	  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
105	- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
106	  session because session data updates continuously.

  "new_string": "- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active\n  session because session data updates continuously.\n\n## Prose form\n\nLong-lived prose on this project follows one shape: a short intro\nthat explains the *why* or the high-level *what*, then a `-` bullet\nlist for the details. Wrap lines at ≤72 cols (bullet continuations\nindent two spaces). Avoid wall-of-prose paragraphs — they hide the\nstructure that bullets make scannable.\n\nSurfaces that use this shape:\n\n- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files —\n  see [Doc comments](#doc-comments-on-every-file-function-and-method).\n- Commit message bodies (both app-repo and session-repo). The\n  ≤50-col title is the commit-specific add-on; see\n  [Commit Message Style](#commit-message-style).\n- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md` — see\n  [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).\n- Todo and Done entries in `notes/todo.md` when an entry needs more\n  than one line of detail. Pure one-liners are still fine.\n\nBullet *content* differs by surface:\n\n- **Commit bodies** — bullets are file-by-file: one bullet per file\n  changed, file plus a one-line gist (e.g.\n  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Source of truth for the\n  mechanical edit list.\n- **Chores / todo / done** — bullets are conceptual (design points,\n  structural notes, the \"what landed and why\" at a notch above\n  file-list granularity). Never a copy of the commit's edit list —\n  see [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).\n- **Doc comments** — bullets are whatever structure fits (fields,\n  cases, invariants).\n\n## Commit Message Style"
}

  "old_string": "### Doc comments on every file, function, and method\n\nEvery `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every\nfunction and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —\none sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the\ncomment exists, not that it be long.\n\nThis is a deliberate override of the generic \"write no comments\"\ndefault that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the\nmodule / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments\ninside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a\nnon-obvious WHY.\n\n**Shape:** short intro (1–3 sentences, shorter is better), then\na `-` bullet list for any structured details. Avoid long prose\nparagraphs — they read as a wall of text and hide the structure\nthat bullets make scannable. Same shape applies to:\n\n- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.\n- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.\n- Todo entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an item needs more than\n  one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).\n\n**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive\n`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets\ninto running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on\nany field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on\nits own line in the rendered help.",
  "new_string": "### Doc comments on every file, function, and method\n\nEvery `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every\nfunction and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —\none sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the\ncomment exists, not that it be long. Doc comments follow the\n[Prose form](#prose-form) shape (intro + bullets).\n\nThis is a deliberate override of the generic \"write no comments\"\ndefault that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the\nmodule / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments\ninside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a\nnon-obvious WHY.\n\n**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive\n`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets\ninto running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on\nany field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on\nits own line in the rendered help."
}

Now you can see the seam visually: bullets 1–4 are the `-s/--scope` docs rollout; bullet 5 is the prose-form unification. They share no semantic ground — the prose-form work emerged from process drift caught while writing the `-2.3` chores section.

**Recommendation: split into `-2.3` + `-2.4`.**

- **`-2.3`** = the original docs sweep: `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `notes/` sweep, `notes/chores-10.md` `-2.3` section (no Design subsection), `notes/todo.md` marker update, `Cargo.toml` → `0.49.0-2.3`.
- **`-2.4`** = the prose-form unification: `CLAUDE.md` (new `## Prose form` + the three slim-downs), `notes/chores-10.md` adds `## chore: prose-form unification (0.49.0-2.4)` with the Design subsection, `notes/todo.md` adds a `-2.4 (current)` ladder bullet, `Cargo.toml` → `0.49.0-2.4`.

**Why split:**

- Bisection clarity — one logical change per sub-step.
- The chores narrative reads cleanly: `## docs: -s/--scope sweep` is the rollout completion; `## chore: prose-form unification` is the separate process improvement.
- Either is bisectable to a small surface (no mixed-cause regressions to untangle).
- The cost is only mechanical: split the WC before the next commit.

**Why not split (one-paragraph case for keeping in `-2.3`):**

- The two halves were edited together; no actual commit yet, so the working copy already has them mixed. Splitting is real work.
- The "docs" `<type>:` on `-2.3` would honestly cover the CLAUDE.md change too — it's all documentation, just two themes.

**Mechanics if you say "split":** I'd revert the prose-form bits in the WC (CLAUDE.md, the `### Design:` subsection in chores-10, the prose-form bullet in todo.md), run cargo cycle, propose `-2.3`'s title/body for push approval. After `-2.3` lands, re-apply the prose-form changes, bump Cargo.toml to `0.49.0-2.4`, add the `-2.4` chores section + ladder bullet, run cargo cycle, propose `-2.4`'s title/body for push.

My vote: split. Your call.

  "old_string": "- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active\n  session because session data updates continuously.\n\n## Prose form\n\nLong-lived prose on this project follows one shape: a short intro\nthat explains the *why* or the high-level *what*, then a `-` bullet\nlist for the details. Wrap lines at ≤72 cols (bullet continuations\nindent two spaces). Avoid wall-of-prose paragraphs — they hide the\nstructure that bullets make scannable.\n\nSurfaces that use this shape:\n\n- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files —\n  see [Doc comments](#doc-comments-on-every-file-function-and-method).\n- Commit message bodies (both app-repo and session-repo). The\n  ≤50-col title is the commit-specific add-on; see\n  [Commit Message Style](#commit-message-style).\n- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md` — see\n  [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).\n- Todo and Done entries in `notes/todo.md` when an entry needs more\n  than one line of detail. Pure one-liners are still fine.\n\nBullet *content* differs by surface:\n\n- **Commit bodies** — bullets are file-by-file: one bullet per file\n  changed, file plus a one-line gist (e.g.\n  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Source of truth for the\n  mechanical edit list.\n- **Chores / todo / done** — bullets are conceptual (design points,\n  structural notes, the \"what landed and why\" at a notch above\n  file-list granularity). Never a copy of the commit's edit list —\n  see [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).\n- **Doc comments** — bullets are whatever structure fits (fields,\n  cases, invariants).\n\n## Commit Message Style",

Doc comments follow the\n[Prose form](#prose-form) shape (intro + bullets).\n\nThis is a deliberate override of the generic \"write no comments\"\ndefault that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the\nmodule / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments\ninside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a\nnon-obvious WHY.\n\n**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive\n`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets\ninto running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on\nany field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on\nits own line in the rendered help.",
  "new_string": "### Doc comments on every file, function, and method\n\nEvery `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every\nfunction and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —\none sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the\ncomment exists, not that it be long.\n\nThis is a deliberate override of the generic \"write no comments\"\ndefault that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the\nmodule / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments\ninside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a\nnon-obvious WHY.\n\n**Shape:** short intro (1–3 sentences, shorter is better), then\na `-` bullet list for any structured details. Avoid long prose\nparagraphs — they read as a wall of text and hide the structure\nthat bullets make scannable. Same shape applies to:\n\n- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.\n- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.\n- Todo entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an item needs more than\n  one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).\n\n**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive\n`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets\ninto running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on\nany field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on\nits own line in the rendered help."
}

lg good, but why undo the CLAUDE change? I would have thought you would have "stashed" it in another jj change, so why delete it?

> AGENT

Fair call — I went linear (revert now, re-apply later) when jj makes it easy to keep multiple WIPs in flight.

The honest reasoning, split into two parts:

**The entangled bits *had* to be reverted either way** — the CLAUDE.md mention inside the `-2.3` chores intro and the prose-form bullet in `-2.3`'s todo entry weren't "stash candidates," they were content that belongs to `-2.3`'s description only after the prose-form work moves out. Reverting those was just cleanup.

**The pure additions could have been stashed** — the new `## Prose form` section in CLAUDE.md, the three slim-downs, the `### Design: Prose form unification` subsection in chores-10. None of those overlap `-2.3`'s edits. I could have `jj split` (or `jj new -B @` + move-the-additions-down) to land them as a separate `-2.4` commit sitting *above* `-2.3` in the chain, then pushed only `-2.3` first. That's the jj-native flow you're describing, and you're right it's cleaner.

Practically, nothing is actually lost — the content is in:

- **My conversation context** (the Edit tool calls show the exact `old_string`/`new_string` of every edit).
- **`jj op log`** for the app repo — every WC mutation is recorded; `jj op restore <op>` could resurrect any prior state. The CLAUDE.md + chores-10 Design subsection are in there as recently-undone states.

So re-applying for `-2.4` is fast either way. But your point stands: the jj-native move would have been to never delete in the first place. I'll lean into `jj split` (or sibling commits) the next time this comes up.

Want me to proceed with the `-2.3` push as planned, or pause to recover the prose-form work as a stashed `-2.4` commit *before* the `-2.3` push (via `jj op restore` or by re-applying it onto a child commit of `@`)? Either works; the re-apply is small.

  "new_string": "- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active\n  session because session data updates continuously.\n\n## Prose form\n\nLong-lived prose on this project follows one shape: a short intro\nthat explains the *why* or the high-level *what*, then a `-` bullet\nlist for the details. Wrap lines at ≤72 cols (bullet continuations\nindent two spaces). Avoid wall-of-prose paragraphs — they hide the\nstructure that bullets make scannable.\n\nSurfaces that use this shape:\n\n- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files —\n  see [Doc comments](#doc-comments-on-every-file-function-and-method).\n- Commit message bodies (both app-repo and session-repo). The\n  ≤50-col title is the commit-specific add-on; see\n  [Commit Message Style](#commit-message-style).\n- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md` — see\n  [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).\n- Todo and Done entries in `notes/todo.md` when an entry needs more\n  than one line of detail. Pure one-liners are still fine.\n\nBullet *content* differs by surface:\n\n- **Commit bodies** — bullets are file-by-file: one bullet per file\n  changed, file plus a one-line gist (e.g.\n  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Source of truth for the\n  mechanical edit list.\n- **Chores / todo / done** — bullets are conceptual (design points,\n  structural notes, the \"what landed and why\" at a notch above\n  file-list granularity). Never a copy of the commit's edit list —\n  see [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).\n- **Doc comments** — bullets are whatever structure fits (fields,\n  cases, invariants).\n\n## Commit Message Style"
}

The version suffix counts toward the\n  50 — the descriptive part gets whatever's left after `<type>: `\n  and ` (<version>)`, so favor terse phrasings (`port X to Context`\n  over `X → Context + XParams`) when the names run long. Common\n  types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`.\n- **Body**: [Prose form](#prose-form) shape (intro + bullets), wrap\n  ≤72 (the \"72\" of the 50/72 rule). App-repo bullets are\n  **file-by-file** — one bullet per file changed, file plus a\n  one-line gist. This list **is the source of truth** for the\n  cycle's mechanical change record; chores carries the narrative +\n  design, not a copy of it. Promote any \"why\" beyond one sentence\n  to a chores `###` subsection rather than expanding the intro.\n- **Session-repo body**: same shape; bullets describe in-session\n  activity rather than code changes.\n- Examples:"
}

Keep them brief —\none sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the\ncomment exists, not that it be long.\n\nThis is a deliberate override of the generic \"write no comments\"\ndefault that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the\nmodule / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments\ninside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a\nnon-obvious WHY.\n\n**Shape:** short intro (1–3 sentences, shorter is better), then\na `-` bullet list for any structured details. Avoid long prose\nparagraphs — they read as a wall of text and hide the structure\nthat bullets make scannable. Same shape applies to:\n\n- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.\n- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.\n- Todo entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an item needs more than\n  one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).\n\n**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive\n`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets\ninto running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on\nany field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on\nits own line in the rendered help.",

1	//! `chid` subcommand — print the short change ID of each commit in a
2	//! revision range, one per line (script-friendly output).
3	//!
4	//! - `ChidArgs`: clap surface; flattens
5	//!   `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`.
6	//! - `chid(&ChidArgs)`: the op — `resolve_spec` / `resolve_header` /
7	//!   `for_each_repo` + print `format_chid`.
8	
9	use clap::Args;
10	use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
11	use log::{debug, info};
12	
13	use crate::common;
14	use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;
15	
16	/// CLI args for `chid` — just the shared read-only commit-query args.
17	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
18	pub struct ChidArgs {
19	    #[command(flatten)]
20	    pub common: CommonArgs,
21	}
22	
23	/// Print the short change ID of each commit in the resolved range.
24	pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
25	    debug!("chid: enter");
26	    let c = &args.common;
27	    let spec = common::resolve_spec(c.pos_rev.as_deref(), c.pos_count, &c.revision, c.limit, "@");
28	    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&c.label, c.no_label);
29	    let repos = c.resolve_repos()?;
30	
31	    common::for_each_repo(&repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {
32	        let (ids, _) =
33	            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;
34	
35	        for commit_id in &ids {
36	            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
37	            info!("{}", common::format_chid(&commit));
38	        }
39	        Ok(())
40	    })?;
41	    debug!("chid: exit");
42	    Ok(())
43	}
44	
45	#[cfg(test)]
46	mod tests {
47	    use std::path::PathBuf;
48	
49	    use clap::Parser;
50	
51	    use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;
52	    use crate::{Cli, Commands};
53	
54	    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> CommonArgs {
55	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap();
56	        match cli.command {
57	            Commands::Chid(a) => a.common,
58	            _ => panic!("expected Chid"),
59	        }
60	    }
61	
62	    #[test]
63	    fn defaults() {
64	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid"]);
65	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@");
66	        assert_eq!(c.repo, None);
67	        assert_eq!(c.scope, None);
68	        assert_eq!(c.limit, None);
69	    }
70	
71	    #[test]
72	    fn with_revision() {
73	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-r", "@-"]);
74	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@-");
75	    }
76	
77	    #[test]
78	    fn with_repo() {
79	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-R", ".claude"]);
80	        assert_eq!(c.repo, Some(PathBuf::from(".claude")));
81	    }
82	
83	    #[test]
84	    fn with_scope_code() {
85	        use crate::scope::{Scope, Side};
86	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-s", "code"]);
87	        assert_eq!(c.scope, Some(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])));
88	    }
89	
90	    #[test]
91	    fn with_scope_code_bot() {
92	        use crate::scope::{Scope, Side};
93	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-s", "code,bot"]);
94	        assert_eq!(c.scope, Some(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])));
95	    }
96	
97	    #[test]
98	    fn with_repo_and_scope_compose() {
99	        // `-R` and `-s` compose: the path is the workspace root, the
100	        // roles are resolved within it. Both fields parse cleanly.
101	        use crate::scope::{Scope, Side};
102	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-R", "../foo", "-s", "bot"]);
103	        assert_eq!(c.repo, Some(PathBuf::from("../foo")));
104	        assert_eq!(c.scope, Some(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot])));
105	    }
106	
107	    #[test]
108	    fn scope_path_rejected_with_hint() {
109	        // Path-via-`-s` is a planned feature; today rejected with a
110	        // hint pointing at `-R`/`--repo`.
111	        let err = crate::Cli::try_parse_from(["vc-x1", "chid", "-s", "./foo"])
112	            .unwrap_err()
113	            .to_string();
114	        assert!(err.contains("path"), "got: {err}");
115	        assert!(err.contains("--repo"), "got: {err}");
116	    }
117	
118	    #[test]
119	    fn with_limit() {
120	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-n", "5"]);
121	        assert_eq!(c.limit, Some(5));
122	    }

121	/// `SquashSpec` by clap). The `--log` path lives on `Context`,
122	/// not here.
123	#[derive(Debug)]
124	pub struct FinalizeParams {
125	    pub repo: PathBuf,
126	    pub squash: Option<SquashSpec>,
127	    pub delay_secs: f64,
128	    pub bookmark: Option<String>,
129	    pub push: bool,

134	impl TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs> for FinalizeParams {
135	    type Error = String;
136	
137	    /// Derive `push`/`bookmark` from `--push` and canonicalize
138	    /// `--repo` (so the detached child resolves it regardless of
139	    /// cwd); `--squash` was already parsed by clap.
140	    fn try_from(a: &FinalizeArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {
141	        let repo = std::fs::canonicalize(&a.repo)
142	            .map_err(|e| format!("cannot resolve repo path '{}': {e}", a.repo.display()))?;
143	        Ok(FinalizeParams {
144	            repo,
145	            squash: a.squash.value.clone(),
146	            delay_secs: a.delay,
147	            bookmark: a.push.clone(),
148	            push: a.push.is_some(),
149	            detach: a.detach,
150	            exec: a.exec,
151	        })
152	    }
153	}

25	/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
26	///
27	/// Counts are `Some(0)` for closed sides and `None` for open (dotted) sides.
28	/// Open sides become unlimited unless a positional count is applied later.
29	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
30	pub struct DotSpec {
31	    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
32	    pub rev: String,
33	    /// Descendant count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
34	    pub desc_count: Option<usize>,
35	    /// Ancestor count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
36	    pub anc_count: Option<usize>,
37	}
38	
39	/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
40	///
41	/// Returns the bare revision with open/closed counts per side.
42	/// `None` means the side had dots (open for expansion);
43	/// `Some(0)` means no dots on that side (closed).
44	/// Actual counts are applied later from positional args.
45	pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
46	    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
47	        DotSpec {
48	            rev: inner.to_string(),
49	            desc_count: None,
50	            anc_count: None,
51	        }
52	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
53	        DotSpec {
54	            rev: inner.to_string(),
55	            desc_count: None,
56	            anc_count: Some(0),
57	        }
58	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
59	        DotSpec {
60	            rev: inner.to_string(),
61	            desc_count: Some(0),
62	            anc_count: None,
63	        }
64	    } else {
65	        DotSpec {
66	            rev: rev.to_string(),
67	            desc_count: Some(0),
68	            anc_count: Some(0),
69	        }
70	    }
71	}
72	
73	/// Resolve positional and flag args into a `DotSpec` ready for `collect_ids`.
74	///
75	/// Handles: dot notation parsing, flag vs positional precedence,
76	/// count-means-total semantics (subtracts 1 for anchor).
77	pub fn resolve_spec(
78	    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
79	    pos_count: Option<usize>,
80	    flag_rev: &str,
81	    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
82	    default_rev: &str,
83	) -> DotSpec {
84	    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
85	    let rev_str = if flag_rev_set {
86	        flag_rev
87	    } else {
88	        pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev) // OK: positional absent → fall back to CLI default
89	    };
90	    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
91	
92	    let count = if flag_limit.is_some() {
93	        flag_limit
94	    } else {
95	        pos_count
96	    };
97	
98	    if let Some(c) = count {
99	        let n = c.saturating_sub(1);
100	        let both_open = spec.desc_count.is_none() && spec.anc_count.is_none();
101	        if both_open {
102	            // Split budget across both sides: ancestors get the extra
103	            let desc_n = n / 2;
104	            let anc_n = n - desc_n;
105	            spec.desc_count = Some(desc_n);
106	            spec.anc_count = Some(anc_n);
107	        } else {
108	            if spec.desc_count.is_none() {
109	                spec.desc_count = Some(n);
110	            }
111	            if spec.anc_count.is_none() {
112	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
113	            }
114	            // bare `x 5` → treat as `x.. 5` (ancestors)
115	            if spec.desc_count == Some(0) && spec.anc_count == Some(0) {
116	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
117	            }
118	        }
119	    }
120	
121	    spec
122	}
123

670	/// - neither flag → `[.]` (today's no-arg default).
671	/// - `-R PATH` alone → `[PATH]` (today's `-R <path>` behavior;
672	///   workspace context not consulted).
673	/// - `-s ROLES` alone → resolves against `find_workspace_root()` —
674	///   the surrounding workspace if there is one, else POR
675	///   (`Side::Code` → `.`, `Side::Bot` → error).
676	/// - `-R PATH -s ROLES` → resolves with `PATH` as the workspace root
677	///   (overrides `find_workspace_root`). This is the composing case;
678	///   e.g. `chid -R ../foo -s bot` queries `../foo/.claude`.
679	///
680	/// `--scope` is keyword-only today (the `parse_scope_roles` value
681	/// parser rejects path forms); the planned `-s <path>` /
682	/// `-s <path>,roles` workspace-root override is a future feature.
683	pub fn resolve_repos(
684	    repo: Option<&Path>,
685	    scope: Option<&Scope>,
686	) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
687	    match (repo, scope) {
688	        (None, None) => Ok(vec![PathBuf::from(".")]),
689	        (Some(p), None) => Ok(vec![p.to_path_buf()]),
690	        (Some(p), Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, Some(p)),
691	        (None, Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, find_workspace_root().as_deref()),
692	    }
693	}
694	
695	#[cfg(test)]
696	mod tests;
```

  "old_string": "pub fn resolve_repos(\n    repo: Option<&Path>,\n    scope: Option<&Scope>,\n) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match (repo, scope) {\n        (None, None) => Ok(vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]),\n        (Some(p), None) => Ok(vec![p.to_path_buf()]),\n        (Some(p), Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, Some(p)),\n        (None, Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, find_workspace_root().as_deref()),\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests;",
  "new_string": "pub fn resolve_repos(\n    repo: Option<&Path>,\n    scope: Option<&Scope>,\n) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match (repo, scope) {\n        (None, None) => Ok(vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]),\n        (Some(p), None) => Ok(vec![p.to_path_buf()]),\n        (Some(p), Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, Some(p)),\n        (None, Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, find_workspace_root().as_deref()),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Resolved shared params for the read-only commit-query subcommands\n/// (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`). Built once at the binary edge\n/// from `CommonArgs` via `TryFrom`; each subcommand's `XxxParams`\n/// embeds this.\n///\n/// - `spec` — resolved `DotSpec` (parsed `..` notation + per-side\n///   count budget).\n/// - `header` — resolved inter-repo `Header` (label vs none).\n/// - `repos` — resolved repo paths (`-R` + `-s` → `Vec<PathBuf>`).\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct CommonParams {\n    pub spec: DotSpec,\n    pub header: Header,\n    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,\n}\n\nimpl TryFrom<&CommonArgs> for CommonParams {\n    type Error = String;\n\n    /// Resolve clap `CommonArgs` into clap-free `CommonParams`: run\n    /// `resolve_spec` / `resolve_header` / `resolve_repos` at the\n    /// binary edge. Fallible because `resolve_repos` can fail\n    /// (workspace lookup, path issues).\n    fn try_from(a: &CommonArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {\n        let spec = resolve_spec(\n            a.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n            a.pos_count,\n            &a.revision,\n            a.limit,\n            \"@\",\n        );\n        let header = resolve_header(&a.label, a.no_label);\n        let repos = a.resolve_repos().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;\n        Ok(CommonParams {\n            spec,\n            header,\n            repos,\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests;"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/chid.rs",
  "old_string": "//! `chid` subcommand — print the short change ID of each commit in a\n//! revision range, one per line (script-friendly output).\n//!\n//! - `ChidArgs`: clap surface; flattens\n//!   `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`.\n//! - `chid(&ChidArgs)`: the op — `resolve_spec` / `resolve_header` /\n//!   `for_each_repo` + print `format_chid`.\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse jj_lib::repo::Repo;\nuse log::{debug, info};\n\nuse crate::common;\nuse crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;\n\n/// CLI args for `chid` — just the shared read-only commit-query args.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct ChidArgs {\n    #[command(flatten)]\n    pub common: CommonArgs,\n}\n\n/// Print the short change ID of each commit in the resolved range.\npub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"chid: enter\");\n    let c = &args.common;\n    let spec = common::resolve_spec(c.pos_rev.as_deref(), c.pos_count, &c.revision, c.limit, \"@\");\n    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&c.label, c.no_label);\n    let repos = c.resolve_repos()?;\n\n    common::for_each_repo(&repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {\n        let (ids, _) =\n            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;\n\n        for commit_id in &ids {\n            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n            info!(\"{}\", common::format_chid(&commit));\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    })?;\n    debug!(\"chid: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}",
  "new_string": "//! `chid` subcommand — print the short change ID of each commit in a\n//! revision range, one per line (script-friendly output).\n//!\n//! - `ChidArgs`: clap surface; flattens\n//!   `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`.\n//! - `ChidParams`: clap-free; embeds `common::CommonParams`. Built\n//!   via `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>` at the binary edge.\n//! - `chid(&Context, &ChidParams)`: the op — `for_each_repo` + print\n//!   `format_chid`.\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse jj_lib::repo::Repo;\nuse log::{debug, info};\n\nuse crate::common::{self, CommonParams};\nuse crate::context::Context;\nuse crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;\n\n/// CLI args for `chid` — just the shared read-only commit-query args.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct ChidArgs {\n    #[command(flatten)]\n    pub common: CommonArgs,\n}\n\n/// Clap-free params for `chid`; embeds resolved `CommonParams`.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct ChidParams {\n    pub common: CommonParams,\n}\n\nimpl TryFrom<&ChidArgs> for ChidParams {\n    type Error = String;\n\n    /// Resolve clap `ChidArgs` into `ChidParams` by delegating to\n    /// `CommonParams::try_from`; `chid` has no fields beyond\n    /// `CommonArgs`.\n    fn try_from(a: &ChidArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {\n        Ok(ChidParams {\n            common: CommonParams::try_from(&a.common)?,\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n/// Print the short change ID of each commit in the resolved range.\n///\n/// `_ctx` is unused (chid has no user-config or `--log` consumer);\n/// it's present for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.\npub fn chid(_ctx: &Context, params: &ChidParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"chid: enter\");\n    let c = &params.common;\n    common::for_each_repo(&c.repos, &c.header, |workspace, repo| {\n        let (ids, _) =\n            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &c.spec.rev, c.spec.desc_count, c.spec.anc_count)?;\n        for commit_id in &ids {\n            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n            info!(\"{}\", common::format_chid(&commit));\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    })?;\n    debug!(\"chid: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-10.md",
  "old_string": "The chosen form also distinguishes bullet *content* per\nsurface — commit-body bullets are file-by-file (the source of\ntruth for the mechanical edit list — `git show` is the record);\nchores / todo / done bullets are conceptual (design points,\nstructural notes — never a copy of the commit's edit list);\ndoc-comment bullets are whatever structure fits.\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "The chosen form also distinguishes bullet *content* per\nsurface — commit-body bullets are file-by-file (the source of\ntruth for the mechanical edit list — `git show` is the record);\nchores / todo / done bullets are conceptual (design points,\nstructural notes — never a copy of the commit's edit list);\ndoc-comment bullets are whatever structure fits.\n\n## refactor: chid → Context+Params (0.49.0-3)\n\nFirst of the four `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` Context+Params\nports (the chid leg of the cycle's Context+Params half — see\n`### Context+Params port` at the top). The Args/Params layering\narrives at the read-only commit-query subcommands and introduces\nthe shared `CommonParams` the next three reuse.\n\n- `common::CommonParams` (flat, clap-free): resolved `DotSpec` +\n  `Header` + `repos: Vec<PathBuf>`. `impl TryFrom<&CommonArgs>`\n  does the `resolve_spec` / `resolve_header` / `resolve_repos`\n  work at the binary edge — fallible because `resolve_repos`\n  can fail (workspace lookup, path issues).\n- `chid::ChidParams`: flat struct embedding `CommonParams`,\n  nothing else (chid has no fields beyond `CommonArgs`).\n  `impl TryFrom<&ChidArgs>` delegates to\n  `CommonParams::try_from`.\n- `pub fn chid(_ctx: &Context, params: &ChidParams)` — `ctx`\n  unused (uniform-signature placeholder, as in\n  `symlink` / `validate-desc` / `fix-desc`).\n- `main.rs` dispatch builds `Context::load(cli.log)` +\n  `ChidParams::try_from(&chid_args)` (mirrors the `Finalize`\n  arm, the other `TryFrom` site).\n- Tests: existing `ChidArgs` parse tests untouched; new\n  `params_from_args_defaults` exercises the boundary\n  resolution.\n\n### Design: error type and import direction\n\nTwo small calls worth recording.\n\n- **Error type `String`** on `TryFrom<&CommonArgs>` matches\n  `finalize` (the existing fallible precedent), not\n  `Box<dyn std::error::Error>`. The actual fallibility is\n  `resolve_repos`'s `Box<dyn Error>` — coerced via\n  `map_err(|e| e.to_string())` at the boundary. `String` keeps\n  the param-construction error surface uniform across the four\n  ports.\n- **`common.rs` now imports\n  `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`** to write the\n  `TryFrom` impl — a new edge from `common` to `options_flags`.\n  `options_flags/common_args.rs` already imports\n  `crate::common::resolve_repos`, so the two modules now\n  cross-reference within the crate. Not a layering inversion:\n  `CommonParams` is the resolved/domain side, `CommonArgs` is\n  the CLI-surface side, and the conversion is a one-way edge\n  from CLI to domain. Defining the impl next to `CommonParams`\n  (the target) is the natural place.\n\n# References"
}

> AGENT

`-3` work complete. Seven files in `@`.

First chid/desc/list/show Context+Params port — introduces the shared `CommonParams` that `desc` / `list` / `show` will reuse in `-4` / `-5` / `-6`.

- **`src/common.rs`** — new `CommonParams` (flat: resolved `DotSpec` + `Header` + `repos`); `impl TryFrom<&CommonArgs>` does the boundary resolution (`resolve_spec` + `resolve_header` + `resolve_repos`); new `use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`.
- **`src/chid.rs`** — new `ChidParams` (embeds `CommonParams`, nothing else); `impl TryFrom<&ChidArgs>` delegates; `pub fn chid` ported to `(_ctx: &Context, params: &ChidParams)`; new `params_from_args_defaults` test exercises the boundary.
- **`src/main.rs`** — `Commands::Chid` arm builds `Context::load(cli.log)` and `ChidParams::try_from(&chid_args)?`, mirroring the `Finalize` arm.
- **`Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock`** — bump `0.49.0-2.4` → `0.49.0-3`.
- **`notes/chores-10.md`** — Refactor tracking table flipped (`chid` → done); new `## refactor: chid → Context+Params (0.49.0-3)` section with `### Design: error type and import direction` subsection (records two small calls: `String` error type to match `finalize`, and the new `common ← options_flags` import edge).
- **`notes/todo.md`** — `-3` marked `(done)`.

Cargo cycle clean: fmt / clippy / test (15 chid parse tests + new `params_from_args_defaults` + the other 5 tests, all pass) / install. Dogfood: `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` and `vc-x1 chid -r @-` both work as before.

1	//! `desc` subcommand — print each commit in a revision range with
2	//! its full description (changeID + commitID + bookmarks + title,
3	//! then the body), the anchor commit bolded.
4	//!
5	//! - `DescArgs`: clap surface; flattens
6	//!   `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`.
7	//! - `desc(&DescArgs)`: the op — `resolve_spec` / `resolve_header` /
8	//!   `for_each_repo` + `format_commit_full` + `indent_body`.
9	
10	use clap::Args;
11	use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
12	use log::{debug, info};
13	
14	use crate::common;
15	use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;
16	
17	/// CLI args for `desc` — just the shared read-only commit-query args.
18	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
19	pub struct DescArgs {
20	    #[command(flatten)]
21	    pub common: CommonArgs,
22	}
23	
24	/// Print each commit in the resolved range with its full description.
25	pub fn desc(args: &DescArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
26	    debug!("desc: enter");
27	    let c = &args.common;
28	    let spec = common::resolve_spec(c.pos_rev.as_deref(), c.pos_count, &c.revision, c.limit, "@");
29	    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&c.label, c.no_label);
30	    let repos = c.resolve_repos()?;
31	
32	    common::for_each_repo(&repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {
33	        let (ids, anchor_index) =
34	            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;
35	
36	        for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
37	            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
38	            let bookmarks = common::format_bookmarks_at(repo, commit_id);
39	            let line = common::format_commit_full(&commit, &bookmarks);
40	            let indented = common::indent_body(&line, 4);
41	            if i == anchor_index {
42	                info!("{}", common::bold_first_line(&indented));
43	            } else {
44	                info!("{indented}");
45	            }
46	        }
47	        Ok(())
48	    })?;
49	    debug!("desc: exit");
50	    Ok(())
51	}
52	
53	#[cfg(test)]
54	mod tests {
55	    use std::path::PathBuf;
56	
57	    use clap::Parser;
58	
59	    use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;
60	    use crate::{Cli, Commands};
61	
62	    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> CommonArgs {
63	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap();
64	        match cli.command {
65	            Commands::Desc(a) => a.common,
66	            _ => panic!("expected Desc"),
67	        }
68	    }
69	
70	    #[test]
71	    fn defaults() {
72	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc"]);
73	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@");
74	        assert_eq!(c.repo, None);
75	        assert_eq!(c.scope, None);
76	    }
77	
78	    #[test]
79	    fn with_revision() {
80	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "-r", "wmuxkqwu"]);
81	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "wmuxkqwu");
82	    }
83	
84	    #[test]
85	    fn with_repo() {
86	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "-R", "/tmp"]);
87	        assert_eq!(c.repo, Some(PathBuf::from("/tmp")));
88	    }
89	
90	    #[test]
91	    fn with_scope_code_bot() {
92	        use crate::scope::{Scope, Side};
93	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "-s", "code,bot"]);
94	        assert_eq!(c.scope, Some(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])));
95	    }
96	
97	    #[test]
98	    fn with_limit() {
99	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "-n", "3"]);
100	        assert_eq!(c.limit, Some(3));
101	    }
102	
103	    #[test]
104	    fn positional_rev() {
105	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "@-"]);
106	        assert_eq!(c.pos_rev, Some("@-".to_string()));
107	        assert_eq!(c.pos_count, None);
108	    }
109	
110	    #[test]
111	    fn positional_rev_and_count() {
112	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "@..", "5"]);
113	        assert_eq!(c.pos_rev, Some("@..".to_string()));
114	        assert_eq!(c.pos_count, Some(5));
115	    }
116	
117	    #[test]
118	    fn positional_both_dots() {
119	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "..abcd..", "3"]);
120	        assert_eq!(c.pos_rev, Some("..abcd..".to_string()));
121	        assert_eq!(c.pos_count, Some(3));
122	    }
123	
124	    #[test]
125	    fn all_opts() {
126	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "desc", "-r", "@-", "-R", ".claude", "-n", "5"]);
127	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@-");
128	        assert_eq!(c.repo, Some(PathBuf::from(".claude")));
129	        assert_eq!(c.limit, Some(5));
130	    }
131	}
